def construct_greedy_support(n, m, rng, restarts, smooth_temp):
    best = None; best_score = inf
    for attempt in range(restarts):
       x = zeros; side = zeros; choose first random or greedy
       for added in range(m):
           candidates = np.where(~x)[0]
           if len(candidates) == 0: break
           # compute score increment; use smooth objective maybe sum exp((side+inc)/scale) or max
           if rng.random()<0.3: pick random candidate
           else compute best by updating side for each candidate? O(n * num_candidates * m)
```

Pseudo:
```
def binary_local_search(n, m0, rng, time_budget, min_m=8, max_m=None, T0=...):
   x=init support size m0 random or greedy
   side=compute_side_counts(x)
   best = x copy, best_max etc
   m=m0
   if max_m is None: max_m=min(n, max(16, 2*n//3?))? For score, larger m reduces normalization but increases side counts. Equal random dense side ~m/n? Let's derive: for random m, max side ~ (m^2/n)*? normalized score ~? Actually side count P_l ~ m^2/n * (density)^? For random independent p=m/n, P_l≈(n-l)p^2≈n p^2=m^2/n. Score=2n*(m^2/n)/m^2=2. Equal random dense score ~2. Sparse m≤sqrt(2n) may <2. For m too high, random score ~2; optimized dense maybe ~? uniform full 2. So max_m maybe n//2 enough. But sparse better. Use max_m = max(min_m, n//2).
   temp = max_side * 0.3 maybe.
   for steps while time:
       choose move type probabilities: swap 0.7, add 0.15, remove 0.15
       old_max = max_side; old_score = score(m, old_max)
       apply, new_max, new_score
       if new_score < best: update best
       accept if new_score <= old_score or random < exp((old-new)/temp) if new/old not inf
       revert if reject
       cool temp
```

Approach: For each m, run several blocks:
- Random/greedy init.
- Perform hill climb: repeatedly find a improving swap via random proposals until none in `max_no_improve` (e.g. 5000). Then random perturbation (kick) of size 2-8 swaps and repeat.
- Keep best.
This avoids SA parameter complexity.

But if stuck at maxP=7 and need 6, kick can escape.

Let's plan code structure:
```
import numpy as np
import time
import math

def binary_side_counts(x, n):
    if n <=1: return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.int32)
    xv = x.astype(np.int32)
    conv = np.convolve(xv, xv, mode='full')
    return np.ascontiguousarray(conv[1:n], dtype=np.int32)

def add_update(side, x, p, sign):
    n = len(x)
    if p > 0:
        left = x[p-1::-1]
        if sign == 1: side[:p] += left
        else: side[:p] -= left
    right_len = n - 1 - p
    if right_len > 0:
        right = x[p+1:p+1+right_len]
        if sign == 1: side[:right_len] += right
        else: side[:right_len] -= right
```
Need careful: In Python, `side[:p] += left` where left bool array length p. But if `side[:p]` view and `left` is x slice, if `left` overlaps side? no. If `sign=-1`, `side[:p] -= left`, bool cast. Good.

But there is a subtlety: For remove, x[p] is still True. The left/right slices exclude p, fine. For add, x[p] false. For swap revert, if we remove j after adding it, x[j] true; side includes pairs to i? Wait after swap remove i add j, side includes pairs involving j to all current ones except i (removed). If reject, remove j: update side using x with j true and i false; subtract pairs j to other ones. Then add i: update side using x with i false and j false; add pairs i to other ones (original state without i and j). But original state had i true and j false, and pairs i to other ones; good. However original state also had no pairs j. Fine.

In evaluating candidate, after remove i add j, to revert we remove j add i. But after evaluating candidate, state should original. Need ensure add_update for i uses x with i false and j false, exactly original? Original had i true, j false. Other ones same. Removing i should have subtracted pairs i to others. Re-adding i will add those pairs. i to j? original no j; after remove i, no j; after revert, no j. Good. Candidate had i-j pair if distance? Removed i so no; adding j counts to others not i; so candidate side excludes i-j? Wait if i and j are both selected in candidate, should side include pair i? Worst active endpoint:
```
def random_worst_one(n, x, side, rng):
    if side.size ==0: return rng.integers(n)
    l0 = int(np.argmax(side)) + 1
    # count active endpoints
    # To avoid Python list large, can sample by trial: randomly choose a segment? Simpler build list.
    act = []
    for i in range(n - l0):
       if x[i] and x[i+l0]: act.append(i); act.append(i+l0)
    if act:
       return int(act[int(rng.integers(len(act)))] )
    return None
```

def local_search_fixed_m(n, m, rng, time_limit, max_batch=32, kick_sizes=(1,2,4,8,12,20), max_no_improve=5000):
    if n <=1 or m<=0 or m>=n: ...
    x = greedy_support(n,m,rng) # maybe
    side = binary_side_counts(x,n)
    h = int(side.max()); var = int(side.dot(side))
    best_x = x.copy(); best_side = side.copy(); best_h=best_cost...
    current_h = h; current_var=var
    stall=0; kick_idx=0
    t0=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       # compute worst active list maybe
       worst_lag = int(np.argmax(side)) + 1 if side.size>0 else 1
       active=[]
       if worst_lag <= n-1:
          for i in range(n-worst_lag):
             if x[i] and x[i+worst_lag]: active.append(i); active.append(i+worst_lag)
       B = max_batch
       best_cand = None; best_cand_h=curr_h; best_cand_var=curr_var; found=False
       old_h = current_h; old_var = current_var
       for _ in range(B):
          # choose i
          if active and rng.random() < 0.5:
             i = int(active[int(rng.integers(len(active)))] )
          else:
             i = int(rng.integers(n))
             # ensure x[i] true
             while not x[i]: i=int(rng.integers(n))
          j = int(rng.integers(n))
          while x[j] or j == i: j=int(rng.integers(n))
          # apply
          add_update(side, x, i, -1); x[i]=False
          add_update(side, x, j, +1); x[j]=True
          new_h = int(side.max())
          if new_h < current_h:
             new_var = int(side.dot(side)) # maybe
             if not found or (new_h, new_var) < (best_cand_h, best_cand_var): store
          elif new_h == current_h:
             new_var = int(side.dot(side))
             if new_var < current_var:
                if not found or (new_h, new_var) < ... store
          # revert
          add_update(side, x, j, -1); x[j]=False
          add_update(side, x, i, +1); x[i]=True
       if found:
          # apply best swap
          add_update(side,x,best_i,-1); x[best_i]=False
          add_update(side,x,best_j,+1); x[best_j]=True
          current_h=best_cand_h; current_var=best_cand_var
          if (current_h,current_var) < (best_h,best_var): update best
          stall=0
       else:
          stall += B
          if stall > max_no_improve:
             # kick
             kick = 1
             # increase kick with consecutive stalls
             k = min(12, (stall // max_no_improve) + 1)
             for _ in range(k // 2?) actually swaps
                choose random i one, j zero; apply swap without evaluation
             current_h=int(side.max()); current_var=int(side.dot(side))
             # if kick made worse, okay
             stall=0
             # after kick, maybe restart batch
             if time > limit: break
             # If kicked too bad, reset to best? Could if current_h >> best_h. If current_h > best_h+3, revert to best state to avoid bad.
             if current_h > best_h + 4:
                 x = best_x.copy(); side=best_side.copy(); current_h=best_h; current_var=best_var
    return x, side, best...
```

Define:
```
def add_point(side, x, p):
    n = x.size
    if p > 0:
        side[:p] += x[p-1::-1]
    r = n - 1 - p
    if r > 0:
        side[:r] += x[p+1:p+1+r]

def remove_point(side, x, p):
    n = x.size
    if p > 0:
        side[:p] -= x[p-1::-1]
    r = n - 1 - p
    if r > 0:
        side[:r] -= x[p+1:p+1+r]
```
For add, x[p] should be False; for remove True. x set after.

`side[:p] += x[p-1::-1]` where x bool; `side` int64? Use int64? Counts max <=m, int32 enough. Adding bool to int32 okay. Use int64 to be safe? int32 dot convert? Use int64. Side length small. dot int64. We'll use `np.int32` to save; dot returns int64? okay. But if n large 10000, m maybe 10000, counts ~10000, int32 fine. For n up to 5000, pairs 25M, int32 fine. Use int64? side length up to n, counts up to m, int64 memory fine. Let's use `np.int64` for side to avoid overflow and dot easy. But additions of bool to int64 slower? minor.

Simpler: if n <= 2048 use direct, else FFT. But we likely use 128. Include helper:
```
def binary_side_counts(x):
 n=len(x)
 if n<=1: return zeros(0)
 if n <= 2048:
    conv = np.convolve(x.astype(np.int64), x.astype(np.int64), mode='full')
 else:
    size=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(x.astype(np.float64), size)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size)[:2*n-1]
    conv=np.rint(conv).astype(np.int64)
 return conv[1:n].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
```
For FFT rounding could off by 1; acceptable? For initial side, better direct if n <=512. If n large, rounding maybe slight. Could correct by direct? not.

Greedy support function:
```
def greedy_support(n, m, rng):
   x=np.zeros(n,bool); side=np.zeros(max(n-1,0), int64)
   if m<=0: return x,side
   p=int(rng.integers(n)); x[p]=True
   for count in range(1,m):
      zeros = np.nonzero(~x)[0]
      if zeros.size ==0: break
      # sample if many
      if zeros.size > 40:
         cand = zeros[rng.choice(zeros.size, size=40, replace=False)]
      else: cand=zeros
      best_p=-1; best_h=None; best_var=None
      for p in cand:
         add_point(side, x, p)
         h=int(side.max())
         if best_p==-1:
            best_p=p; best_h=h; best_var=int(side.dot(side))
         else:
            if h < best_h or (h == best_h and int(side.dot(side)) < best_var):
                best_var=...; best_h=h; best_p=p
         remove_point(side, x, p)
      if best_p != -1:
         add_point(side, x, best_p); x[best_p]=True
   return x, side
```
Need ensure when evaluating candidate p, add_point uses x with p false. Good. `side.max()` after adding. If side size 0 (n=1) handle. For n small.

Random support:
```
def random_support(n,m,rng):
   idx = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)
   x=zeros; x[idx]=True; side=binary_side_counts(x)
```

Implement `local_search_fixed_m` with time_limit. Pseudocode refined:
```
def local_search_fixed_m(n, m, rng, time_limit, start_x=None):
    if start_x is None: x,side=greedy_support(n,m,rng)
    else: x=start_x.copy(); side=binary_side_counts(x); m=int(x.sum())
    if m==0 or m>=n: return ...
    current_h = int(side.max()) if side.size else 0
    current_var = int(side.dot(side))
    best_x=x.copy(); best_side=side.copy(); best_h=current_h; best_var=current_var
    t0=time.monotonic()
    stall=0
    kick_level=0
    max_batch=32
    no_improve_limit=4000
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       # If side size 0 break
       if side.size == 0: break
       # active endpoints for worst lag
       worst_lag = int(np.argmax(side)) + 1
       active = []
       # If m maybe large; active length up to 2*side[worst]
       # Build using vector? For small n Python loop.
       # To speed, use np.nonzero(x[:-worst_lag] & x[worst_lag:])
       if worst_lag < n:
           matches = np.nonzero(x[:-worst_lag] & x[worst_lag:])[0]
           if matches.size:
               active = list(matches) + list(matches + worst_lag)
       found=False
       best_i=-1; best_j=-1; best_ch=current_h; best_cv=current_var
       old_h=current_h; old_var=current_var
       for _ in range(max_batch):
          if active and rng.random() < 0.5:
              i = int(active[int(rng.integers(len(active)))] )
          else:
              i = int(rng.integers(n))
              # ensure one
              while x[i] == False:
                  i = int(rng.integers(n))
          j = int(rng.integers(n))
          while x[j] == True or j == i:
              j = int(rng.integers(n))
          # swap
          remove_point(side, x, i); x[i]=False
          add_point(side, x, j); x[j]=True
          new_h = int(side.max())
          if new_h < old_h or (new_h == old_h):
              if new_h < old_h:
                  new_var = int(side.dot(side))
                  if not found or (new_h, new_var) < (best_ch,best_cv):
                      found=True; best_i=i; best_j=j; best_ch=new_h; best_cv=new_var
              else:
                  new_var = int(side.dot(side))
                  if not found or (new_h, new_var) < (best_ch,best_cv):
                     # Need ensure improvement over current? old_h,old_var
                     if (new_h, new_var) < (old_h, old_var):
                         found=True; best_i=i; best_j=j; best_ch=new_h; best_cv=new_var
          # revert
          remove_point(side, x, j); x[j]=False
          add_point(side, x, i); x[i]=True
       if found:
          remove_point(side,x,best_i); x[best_i]=False
          add_point(side,x,best_j); x[best_j]=True
          current_h=best_ch; current_var=best_cv
          if current_h < best_h or (current_h == best_h and current_var < best_var):
              best_x=x.copy(); best_side=side.copy(); best_h=current_h; best_var=current_var
          stall=0; kick_level=0
       else:
          stall += max_batch
          if stall > no_improve_limit:
              # kick or reset
              kick_level +=1
              # If too far from best, reset to best
              if current_h > best_h + max(2, best_h//4) or kick_level > 6:
                  x = best_x.copy(); side = best_side.copy(); current_h=best_h; current_var=best_var; kick_level=0
              else:
                  k = min(8, kick_level + 1)
                  for _ in range(k):
                     # random one and zero
                     i = int(rng.integers(n))
                     while x[i] == False: i=...
                     j = int(rng.integers(n))
                     while x[j] == True or j == i: j=...
                     remove_point(side,x,i); x[i]=False
                     add_point(side,x,j); x[j]=True
                  current_h=int(side.max()); current_var=int(side.dot(side))
              stall=0
              # time check
              if time.monotonic()-t0 >= time_limit: break
    return best_x, best_side, best_h, best_var
```

Precompute pair indices:
```
def support_pair_matrices(idx, n):
   m=len(idx)
   pos = {idx[i]: i for i}
   pairs_by_lag = [ [] for _ in range(n) ] # l=0..n-1
   for i in range(m):
      for j in range(i+1,m):
         l=abs(idx[i]-idx[j])
         if l>0: pairs_by_lag[l].append((i,j))
   return pairs_by_lag
```

def polish_weights(idx, n, time_budget, rng):
    m=len(idx)
    if m<=1: return
    pairs = build pairs
    w = np.ones(m)/m
    best_w=w; best_sc=support_score
    eta=0.5? Since variables scale 1/m, gradient scale? side values ~1/m^2. Use step 0.1 maybe.
    tau = 0.05*max_side
    iter count until budget (e.g. 5s)
    while time:
       side = bincount(lags, weights=w[I]*w[J])
       cur = side.max()
       tau = max(1e-9, 0.05*cur)
       p=softmax(side[?:]+? tau) include only lags 1..n-1.
       g = bincount(I, p[lags]*w[J]) + ...
       # gradient for log? Objective f= max u, gradient of softmax approx. step eta
       w_new = project_simplex(w - eta*g)
       new_sc=...
       accept if new<cur or rng
       update eta adapt
```

def build_support_pairs(n, idx):
    m = len(idx)
    I=np.empty(0); J=np.empty(0); L=np.empty(0)
    # Since n small, loops ok
    for a in range(m):
       for b in range(a+1,m):
          l = abs(int(idx[a]) - int(idx[b]))
          if l>0:
             append
    return L,I,J
```

Support side values:
```
def support_side_values(w, L, I, J, n):
    val = w[I] * w[J]
    return np.bincount(L, weights=val, minlength=n)[:n-1]
```

`project_simplex`: from previous. Need handle `v` 0d? We'll 1d. Use `v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()`. If n=0 return. Use formula. Ensure sum exactly 1? due rounding, we can `s=v.sum(); if s>0: v/=s`.

`binary_side_counts`:
```
def binary_side_counts(x):
    n = int(x.size)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.int64)
    if n <= 4096:  # direct maybe ok for 4096? 16M ops okay maybe. Could use 2048.
        xv = x.astype(np.int64)
        conv = np.convolve(xv, xv, mode='full')
        out = conv[1:n]
    else:
        size = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
        fa = np.fft.rfft(x.astype(np.float64), size)
        conv = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, size)[:2*n-1]
        conv = np.rint(conv).astype(np.int64)
        out = conv[1:n]
    return out.astype(np.int64, copy=False)
```
For n=4096 direct conv 16M maybe okay but slow if many. We likely n=128. Use threshold 2048 maybe.

`add_point` and `remove_point`: use x.size. Need `side[:p] += x[p-1::-1]`. If `p` can be 0, no. For right, `r = n-1-p`, if r>0: `right = x[p+1:p+1+r]`. Note if p+1+right_len = n. Good.
Add bool array to int64 side: In-place `+=` with bool works? Let's confirm: `a=np.zeros(3,dtype=np.int64); a[:2]+=np.array([True,False])` works. Good.

`random_support`:
```
def random_support(n,m,rng):
    x=np.zeros(n,bool)
    if m > 0 and m < n:
       idx = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)
       x[idx]=True
    elif m == n:
       x[:] = True
    side = binary_side_counts(x)
    return x, side
```
If m=0: side zeros.

`greedy_support`:
```
def greedy_support(n,m,rng):
    x = np.zeros(n,bool); side = np.zeros(max(n-1,0), dtype=np.int64)
    if m <=0: return x,side
    if m >= n:
       x[:] = (m==n); side=binary_side_counts(x); return x,side # but m<n expected
    p = int(rng.integers(n)); x[p]=True
    for _ in range(1,m):
       zeros = np.nonzero(~x)[0]
       if zeros.size ==0: break
       if zeros.size > 48:
          cand = zeros[rng.choice(zeros.size, size=48, replace=False)]
       else:
          cand = zeros
       best_p = -1; best_h = None; best_var = None
       for p in cand:
           add_point(side, x, int(p))
           h = int(side.max())
           var = int(side.dot(side))
           if best_p == -1 or h < best_h or (h == best_h and var < best_var):
               best_p=int(p); best_h=h; best_var=var
           remove_point(side, x, int(p))
       if best_p == -1: break
       add_point(side, x, best_p); x[best_p]=True
    return x, side
```
For `m>=n`, if m=n but local search no zeros. We won't. For m=0, side zero.

After binary search, `idx = np.flatnonzero(x_best)`. If len idx != m? Should be m. If not, use actual. Weight polish:
```
def polish_weights(n, idx, time_budget, rng):
    m = len(idx)
    if m < 2 or time_budget <=0: return None
    L,I,J=build_support_pairs(n, idx)
    if L.size==0: return
    w = np.ones(m)/m
    # maybe initialize with cosine taper? All equal.
    best_w = w.copy(); best_sc = 2*n*support_side_values(w,...).max()
    eta0 = 0.1
    t0=time
    stall=0; step=eta0
    while time.time - t0 < time_budget:
       side = support_side_values(w,L,I,J,n)
       cur = float(side.max())
       if cur < best_sc: update
       tau = max(1e-12, 0.03 * cur)
       z = side/tau; z-=z.max(); p=np.exp(z); p /= p.sum()
       g = np.bincount(I, weights=p[L]*w[J], minlength=m) + np.bincount(J, weights=p[L]*w[I], minlength=m)
       # normalize gradient by m? Step candidates
       best_step_w=None; best_step_sc=None
       for fac in [step, 0.5*step, 0.25*step, 2.0*step]:
          w_cand = project_simplex(w - fac*g)
          sc = 2*n*float(support_side_values(w_cand,...).max())
          if best_step_sc is None or sc < best_step_sc: store
       if best_step_sc < best_sc:
          w=best_step_w; best_sc=best_step_sc; stall=0; step*=1.05
       else:
          stall +=1
          if stall>10: w=best_w.copy(); step*=0.7; stall=0
          if step < 1e-4/np.sqrt(m): break
    return best_w
```
But `support_side_values` called multiple times (line search). S small. Good.
Need ensure `p[L]` where L int. `I,J` int arrays.
`np.bincount` requires weights float, minlength int. It returns float.
Step size: `g` scale? If `m=32`, w=0.031, active p maybe 0.1, degree maybe 2, g~0.006. Step 0.1 gives change 0.0006 small. Too small! Actually gradient should be relative to variable scale? w sum1, derivative of u ~1/m^2? Let's compute: u_l=sum pairs w_i w_j. If w ~1/m, each pair ~1/m^2. Number pairs at active lag ~K. derivative_i = sum_{neighbors active lags p_l * w_j}. p_l sum to 1, so sum over l p_l * degree_l/m. Average degree? Total p weighted degree = (2S/m?) Approximately 2S/m = m-1. So gradient magnitude ~ m/m = O(1)? Wait w_j~1/m, p sum=1, sum over all neighbors for each i of p_l w_j = (1/m)*∑ p_l degree_i_l. ∑ p_l degree_i_l average = (2S/m? maybe m)?? For each i, total degree over all lags m-1. Weighted average degree ≤m-1. So gradient ≤1. More typical 0.1. Step 0.1 good. It changes weights by ~0.1 of variables? If gradient components ~0.1, step 0.1 changes w by 0.01 (relative 30%). okay.
But before project, w - step*g may have negative. Fine.
Could use `g *= m` to scale? no.
Line search relative to current side. Good.

Maybe use a smaller `tau` so active lags stronger. tau=0.05*cur. If cur~0.0058, tau=0.0003; side/tau for active 1, inactive maybe0; p concentrates. Gradient active. Good.

`safe_eval`:
```
def safe_eval(seq):
    try:
       val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
       val = float(val)
       if np.isfinite(val): return val
    except Exception:
       return math.inf
    return math.inf
```

Write `project_simplex`:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    if np.any(~np.isfinite(v)):
        v = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.0)
    if n == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64) / 1.0? But simplex sum1, if one var, should [1.0]? If input length 1 and sum maybe not 1, projection onto {x>=0,sum=1} => [1]. But our w length m could be1. Use [1.0].
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u * idx > (cssv - 1.0)
    rho = np.where(cond)[0][-1] if cond.any() else 0
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1.0) / (rho + 1.0)
    out = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = out.sum()
    if s > 0:
       out /= s
    else:
       out = np.ones(n)/n
    return out
```
If n=1 return [1.0] regardless? But if project of [0] to sum1? In polynomial optimization, sum should be1; if m=1, weight must1. Good.

For `polish_weights`, initial w sum1. Suppose due projection n=1 returns [1]. Good.

`build_support_pairs`: Use int arrays. For m maybe large 512, pair count 130k, ok. But if m=1000, 500k maybe memory. We'll only polish supports up to maybe 128? For n128 m<=64. Good.
Implementation with preallocation? Simpler Python lists append then np.array.
```
def build_support_pairs(n, idx):
    idx = np.asarray(idx, dtype=np.int64)
    m=idx.size
    L=[]; I=[]; J=[]
    for a in range(m):
       for b in range(a+1,m):
          l=int(abs(idx[a]-idx[b]))
          if 0 < l < n:
             L.append(l); I.append(a); J.append(b)
    L=np.array(L,dtype=np.int64); I=... if empty dtype int64
    return L,I,J
```
For m=64 loops 2k fine. If m=256 loops 32k fine.

`support_side_values`:
```
def support_side_values(w, L, I, J, n):
    if L.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(max(n-1,0), dtype=np.float64)
    weights = w[I] * w[J]
    return np.bincount(L, weights=weights, minlength=n)[:n-1]
```
If n maybe large, minlength n. side length n-1. Good.

Let's write helper to make normalized candidate from array:

def normalize_nonnegative(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-12:
       return np.ones(a.size)/max(a.size,1)
    return a/s
```

def clean_sequence_list(a):

   arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
   arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
   return [float(x) for x in arr]

Let's write `get_m_plan` robust:
```
def get_m_plan(n):
    ratios = [0.10,0.12,0.15,0.18,0.20,0.22,0.25,0.28,0.32,0.36,0.40,0.45]
    plan=[]; seen=set()
    for r in ratios:
       m=int(round(n*r))
       m=max(2,min(n-1,m))
       if m in seen: continue
       seen.add(m)
       denom=max(1,n-1)
       S=m*(m-1)//2
       lower=(S+denom-1)//denom
       target_h=int(math.floor((1.5027*m*m - 1e-9)/(2.0*max(1,n))))
       if target_h < lower: continue
       margin=target_h-lower
       w=1.0 + 0.25*float(min(10, margin))
       # Extra for m near 0.2-0.35
       w *= 1.0 + 0.5*math.exp(-((r-0.25)/0.12)**2)
       plan.append((m,w))
    if not plan:
       m=max(2,min(n-1,n//4))
       plan.append((m,1.0))
    return plan
```

   var = int(side.dot(side)) if side.size else 0
   return x, side, h, var
m = m_actual
```

`internal_side_score` for dense:
```
def internal_side_score(a, n):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if n <=1 or a.size <2: return 0.0
    b=np.convolve(a[:n], a[:n], mode='full')
    if b.size <=1: return 0.0
    return 2.0*float(n)*float(np.max(b[1:n]))
```
If a length >n use first n. Good.

Dense refine code:
```
def dense_refine_side(a, n, time_budget, rng):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size != n:
       tmp=np.zeros(n)
       m=min(n,a.size); tmp[:m]=a[:m]; a=tmp
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12:
       return a
    a = project_simplex(a / s)
    if n <= 1 or time_budget <= 0:
       return a
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = internal_side_score(a,n)
    eta=0.08
    beta=0.5
    vel=np.zeros(n)
    stall=0
    t0=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_budget:
       b=np.convolve(a,a,mode='full')
       side=b[1:n]
       cur=2.0*n*float(np.max(side)) if side.size else 0.0
       if cur < best_sc:
           best_sc=cur; best_a=a.copy()
       if side.size ==0: break
       # gradient top lags
       tau=max(1e-12, 0.05*float(np.max(side)))
       z=side/tau
       z=z-z.max()
       probs=np.exp(z)
       psum=probs.sum()
       if psum <=0: probs=np.zeros(side.size); probs[int(np.argmax(side))]=1.0
       else: probs/=psum
       top_count=min(32, int(side.size))
       if top_count == side.size:
          top=np.nonzero(probs > 0)[0]
       else:
          # get top indices
          top=np.argpartition(probs, -top_count)[-top_count:]
       g=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
       for ti in top:
          li=int(ti)
          wl=float(probs[li])
          if wl <= 1e-10: continue
          l=li+1
          g[:n-l] += wl * a[l:]
          g[l:]  += wl * a[:-l]
       vel = beta*vel + (1.0-beta)*g
       # line search
       best_cand=None; best_cand_sc=cur
       for fac in (eta, 0.5*eta, 0.25*eta, 0.1*eta, 2.0*eta):
           cand=project_simplex(a - fac*vel)
           sc=internal_side_score(cand,n)
           if best_cand is None or sc < best_cand_sc:
               best_cand=cand; best_cand_sc=sc
       if best_cand is not None and best_cand_sc < cur - 1e-12:
           a=best_cand
           if best_cand_sc < best_sc: best_sc=best_cand_sc; best_a=a.copy()
           stall=0
           eta=min(1.0, eta*1.05)
       else:
           stall +=1
           if stall > 8:
               a=best_a.copy()
               eta *=0.7
               vel=np.zeros(n)
               stall=0
               if eta < 1e-4: break
    return best_a
```
Use tuple fac in for, okay no lambda. `np.argpartition(probs, -top_count)` if top_count=0? side.size>0. In numpy, kth must be between -M and M-1? I think -M is allowed? Might not. Simpler if top_count == side.size use `np.arange(side.size)`.

```

Let's draft full code now mentally and check syntax.

```
def project_simplex(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    v = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.0)
    if n == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n+1, dtype=np.float64)
    mask = u * idx > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not mask.any():
        rho = 0
    else:
        rho = int(np.where(mask)[0][-1])
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1.0) / (rho + 1.0)
    out = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = out.sum()
    if s > 0:
        out /= s
    else:
        out = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    return out
```
If n=1, projection of any to simplex sum1 returns [1]. Good.

`binary_side_counts`:
```
def binary_side_counts(x):
    n = int(x.size)
    if n <=1:
       return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.int64)
    if n <= 2048:
       xv = x.astype(np.int64)
       conv = np.convolve(xv, xv, mode='full')
       out = conv[1:n]
    else:
       size = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
       fa = np.fft.rfft(x.astype(np.float64), size)
       conv = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size)[:2*n-1]
       conv = np.rint(conv).astype(np.int64)
       out = conv[1:n]
    return np.ascontiguousarray(out, dtype=np.int64)
```
If n huge 100000, FFT size 262144 okay, but x.astype float; conv float. But we likely not. `np.ascontiguousarray(out, dtype=np.int64)` works? It accepts `dtype`? Yes `np.ascontiguousarray(a, dtype=None)`. Good. Or `out.astype(np.int64, copy=False)`. Use latter.

`add_point`:
```
def add_point(side, x, p):
    n = int(x.size)
    if p > 0:
       side[:p] += x[p-1::-1]
    r = n - 1 - p
    if r > 0:
       side[:r] += x[p+1:p+1+r]
```
`side[:p]` length p; `x[p-1::-1]` length p if p>0. Good. If x bool, slice view. `+=` in-place with bool okay. Could if `side` is int64 and x bool, result in-place casting? yes.

`remove_point` similar with `-=`.

`random_support`:
```
def random_support(n,m,rng):
    x=np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)
    if m <=0:
       return x, np.zeros(max(n-1,0), dtype=np.int64)
    if m >= n:
       x[:] = True
    else:
       idx = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)
       x[idx] = True
    side = binary_side_counts(x)
    return x, side
```

`greedy_support` as above. Need if `m >= n`: return full if m=n; but local search m<n. If m>n? cap.
```
def greedy_support(n,m,rng):
    x=np.zeros(n,bool)
    side=np.zeros(max(n-1,0), dtype=np.int64)
    if n <=1:
       if m>0: x[0]=True
       side=binary_side_counts(x)
       return x,side
    m = min(max(0,m), n)
    if m == 0:
       return x, side
    if m == n:
       x[:]=True; side=binary_side_counts(x); return x,side
    p = int(rng.integers(n)); x[p]=True
    for _ in range(1,m):
       zeros = np.nonzero(~x)[0]
       if zeros.size ==0: break
       if zeros.size > 64:
          cand = zeros[rng.choice(zeros.size, size=64, replace=False)]
       else:
          cand = zeros
       best_p=-1; best_h=None; best_var=None
       for cand_p in cand:
           p=int(cand_p)
           add_point(side,x,p)
           h=int(side.max())
           if best_h is None:
               var=int(side.dot(side))
               best_p=p; best_h=h; best_var=var
           elif h < best_h:
               best_p=p; best_h=h; best_var=int(side.dot(side))
           elif h == best_h:
               var=int(side.dot(side))
               if var < best_var:
                   best_p=p; best_h=h; best_var=var
           remove_point(side,x,p)
       if best_p >=0:
           add_point(side,x,best_p)
           x[best_p]=True
       else:
           break
    return x, side
```
For side.max() each evaluation; if side size 0 (n=1) but m<n so n>1. Good.

`initialize_support`:
```
def initialize_support(n,m,rng):
    if m <=0 or n <=1:
       return random_support(n,m,rng)
    if rng.random() < 0.7:
       return greedy_support(n,m,rng)
    return random_support(n,m,rng)
```

`local_search_fixed_m`: Let's write carefully.
```
def local_search_fixed_m(n, m, rng, time_limit, start_x=None):
    if n <= 1:
       if start_x is not None:
          x=np.asarray(start_x,dtype=bool).copy()
       else:
          x=np.zeros(n,bool)
       side=binary_side_counts(x)
       h=0
       var=0
       return x,side,h,var
    if start_x is not None:
       x = np.asarray(start_x, dtype=bool).copy()
       if x.size != n:
          tmp=np.zeros(n,bool); mn=min(n,x.size); tmp[:mn]=x[:mn]; x=tmp
       side = binary_side_counts(x)
    else:
       x, side = initialize_support(n,m,rng)
    m = int(x.sum())
    if m <= 0 or m >= n:
       h = int(side.max()) if side.size else 0
       var = int(side.dot(side)) if side.size else 0
       return x, side, h, var

    current_h = int(side.max()) if side.size else 0
    current_var = int(side.dot(side)) if side.size else 0
    best_x = x.copy(); best_side = side.copy(); best_h=current_h; best_var=current_var
    denom = max(1, n-1)
    S = m*(m-1)//2
    lower_h = (S + denom - 1)//denom

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    stall = 0
    kick_counter = 0
    batch = 24
    no_improve_limit = 2500

    if time_limit <= 0:
       return best_x,best_side,best_h,best_var

    while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit:
       if side.size == 0 or best_h <= lower_h:
           break
       ones = np.flatnonzero(x)
       zeros = np.flatnonzero(~x)
       if ones.size == 0 or zeros.size == 0:
           break
       # active endpoints
       worst_lag = int(np.argmax(side)) + 1
       active = []
       if 1 <= worst_lag < n:
           matches = np.nonzero(x[:-worst_lag] & x[worst_lag:])[0]
           if matches.size:
               active = list(matches) + list(matches + worst_lag)
       old_h = current_h
       old_var = current_var
       found = False
       best_i = -1; best_j = -1; best_ch = old_h; best_cv = old_var
       for _ in range(batch):
           if active and rng.random() < 0.45:
               i = int(active[int(rng.integers(len(active)))])
           else:
               i = int(ones[int(rng.integers(ones.size))])
           j = int(zeros[int(rng.integers(zeros.size))])
           # apply swap
           remove_point(side, x, i); x[i] = False
           add_point(side, x, j); x[j] = True
           new_h = int(side.max())
           if new_h < old_h:
               new_var = int(side.dot(side))
               if (not found) or new_h < best_ch or (new_h == best_ch and new_var < best_cv):
                   found = True; best_i=i; best_j=j; best_ch=new_h; best_cv=new_var
           elif new_h == old_h:
               new_var = int(side.dot(side))
               if (new_h < best_ch) or (new_h == best_ch and new_var < best_cv) and (new_h < old_h or new_var < old_var):
                   # But if found already with h<old, new_h==old not better. Since best_ch may <old, condition handles.
                   if (new_h < best_ch) or (new_h == best_ch and new_var < best_cv):
                       # require improvement over current
                       if (new_h < old_h) or (new_var < old_var):
                           found = True; best_i=i; best_j=j; best_ch=new_h; best_cv=new_var
           # revert
           remove_point(side, x, j); x[j] = False
           add_point(side, x, i); x[i] = True
       if found:
           remove_point(side, x, best_i); x[best_i] = False
           add_point(side, x, best_j); x[best_j] = True
           current_h = best_ch; current_var = best_cv
           if current_h < best_h or (current_h == best_h and current_var < best_var):
               best_h = current_h; best_var=current_var; best_x=x.copy(); best_side=side.copy()
           stall = 0
           kick_counter = 0
       else:
           stall += batch
           if stall > no_improve_limit:
               kick_counter += 1
               need_restart = (kick_counter >= 10) or (current_h > best_h + max(2, best_h // 3))
               if need_restart:
                   x, side = initialize_support(n, m, rng)
                   current_h = int(side.max()) if side.size else 0
                   current_var = int(side.dot(side)) if side.size else 0
                   if current_h < best_h or (current_h == best_h and current_var < best_var):
                       best_h = current_h; best_var=current_var; best_x=x.copy(); best_side=side.copy()
                   kick_counter = 0
               else:
                   k = min(int(ones.size), int(zeros.size), 1 + kick_counter // 2)
                   for _ in range(k):
                       ones_k = np.flatnonzero(x)
                       zeros_k = np.flatnonzero(~x)
                       if ones_k.size == 0 or zeros_k.size == 0:
                           break
                       i = int(ones_k[int(rng.integers(ones_k.size))])
                       j = int(zeros_k[int(rng.integers(zeros_k.size))])
                       remove_point(side, x, i); x[i] = False
                       add_point(side, x, j); x[j] = True
                   if side.size:
                       current_h = int(side.max())
                       current_var = int(side.dot(side))
                       if current_h < best_h or ...: update best? Actually kick may improve; update best if improve.
               stall = 0
    return best_x, best_side, best_h, best_var
```

`polish_weights`: Need if `time_budget <=0` return initial? maybe None. We'll return initial. Code:
```
def polish_weights(n, idx, time_budget, rng):
   m=idx.size
   if m <=1 or time_budget <=0: return np.ones(m)/m if m>0 else None
   L,I,J=build_support_pairs(n, np.asarray(idx,int))
   if L.size ==0:
      return np.ones(m)/m
   w=np.ones(m)/m
   best_w=w.copy()
   best_sc=2.0*n*float(support_side_values(w,L,I,J,n).max())
   step=0.1
   stall=0
   t0=time.monotonic()
   while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_budget:
       side=support_side_values(w,L,I,J,n)
       cur_side=float(side.max())
       cur=2.0*n*cur_side
       if cur < best_sc:
          best_sc=cur; best_w=w.copy()
       if cur_side <= 1e-15: break
       tau=max(1e-15, 0.04*cur_side)
       z=side/tau
       z=z-z.max()
       p=np.exp(z)
       s=p.sum()
       if s <=0:
          p=np.zeros(side.size); p[int(np.argmax(side))]=1.0
       else: p/=s
       pl=p[L]
       g=np.bincount(I, weights=pl*w[J], minlength=m) + np.bincount(J, weights=pl*w[I], minlength=m)
       # if gradient all zero, break
       if not np.any(g): break
       best_cand=None; best_cand_sc=cur
       for fac in (step, 0.5*step, 0.25*step, 0.1*step, 2.0*step):
           wc=project_simplex(w - fac*g)
           sc=2.0*n*float(support_side_values(wc,L,I,J,n).max())
           if best_cand is None or sc < best_cand_sc:
               best_cand=wc; best_cand_sc=sc
       if best_cand is not None and best_cand_sc < cur - 1e-13:
           w=best_cand
           if best_cand_sc < best_sc: best_sc=best_cand_sc; best_w=w.copy()
           stall=0
           step=min(0.5, step*1.05)
       else:
           stall +=1
           if stall > 8:
               w=best_w.copy()
               step *=0.7
               stall=0
               if step < 1e-5: break
   return best_w
```
Use `np.any(g)` returns bool. Good.

`get_m_plan`: Use if n==128 fixed; else ratios.
```
def get_m_plan(n):
    if n == 128:
       return [(20,1.0),(24,1.0),(28,1.2),(32,1.6),(36,1.6),(40,1.2),(48,0.9),(56,0.7),(64,0.6)]
    plan=[]
    ratios=[0.12,0.15,0.18,0.20,0.22,0.25,0.28,0.32,0.36,0.40,0.45]
    seen=set()
    for r in ratios:
       m=int(round(n*r))
       m=max(2,min(n-1,m))
       if m in seen: continue
       seen.add(m)
       S=m*(m-1)//2
       denom=max(1,n-1)
       lower=(S+denom-1)//denom
       target_h=int(math.floor((1.5027*m*m - 1e-9)/(2.0*max(1,n))))
       if target_h < lower: continue
       margin=target_h-lower
       w=1.0 + 0.25*float(min(10,margin))
       w *= 1.0 + 0.5*math.exp(-((r-0.25)/0.12)**2)
       plan.append((m,w))
    if not plan:
       m=max(2,min(n-1,n//4))
       plan.append((m,1.0))
    return plan
```
For n=128 fixed includes m=20 target2 lower2, m=24 target3 lower3, etc. Good.

`clean_sequence_list`:
```
def clean_sequence_list(a):
    arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size ==0: return []
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    return [float(v) for v in arr]
```

`propose_candidate` end:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
   rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
   end = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(budget_s) - 30.0)
   pool=[]
   # add base
   try:
      base = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
      if base.ndim == 1 and base.size <= 10000 and base.size > 0:
         bclean = clean_sequence_list(base)
         if any(v>0 for v in bclean): pool.append(('base', bclean, None))
         # normalized if sum>0
         s=sum(bclean)
         if s > 0:

         remaining_weight -= w

def convolution_matrix(a):
   n = len(a); K=2*n-1; A=np.zeros((K,n), float)
   for k in range(K):
       i_start = max(0, k-(n-1))
       i_end = min(n-1, k)
       j = k - i  # so A[k, i] = a[j]
```

Function `conv_score(a)`:
```
def conv_score(a):
    n=a.size
    b=np.convolve(a,a,'full')
    return float(2*n*np.max(b))
```

Use direct n<=256, FFT otherwise.
```
def full_conv(a):
   n=a.size
   if n <= 256:
       return np.convolve(a,a,mode='full')
   size=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a.astype(np.float64), size)
   return np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size)[:2*n-1]
```
Convolution result positive; FFT rounding tiny negative; max okay. Use for score.

LP loop:
```
def lp_search(a, time_budget, rng):
   a=normalize(a)
   best=a; best_sc=score
   no_improve=0
   while time_left:
      b=full_conv(a); M=b.max(); cur=2nM
      A=build_conv_mat(a)
      res=linprog(-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=M*ones, bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
      if res is None or res.x is None: break
      g=clip(res.x)
      s=g.sum()
      if s<=0: break
      gnorm=g/s
      cand, sc = line_search(a, gnorm, cur)
      if cand is not None and sc < best_sc:
          a=cand; best_sc=sc; best=a; no_improve=0
      else:
          no_improve+=1
          # maybe do a mirror step or perturb
          if no_improve > 5: 
              # fallback to subgradient iteration or random perturbation
              a = mirror_step(a, b, eta=..., rng)
              # or perturb
              if sc? update
              no_improve=0
   return best,best_sc
```
But if no improve, using g line search fails; we can try smaller t; done. Could also try using LP with lower target T = M*(1-1e-3)? Not if infeasible. Or use dual/mirror. We'll implement a `mirror_subgrad_step` as fallback.

Mirror step details:
```
def mirror_step(a, b, rng, tau_frac=0.1, eta=1.0):
   n=a.size
   M=float(b.max())
   if M <= 0: return a, cur
   tau = tau_frac * M
   z=b/tau; z-=z.max(); lam=exp(z); lam/=sum
   # gradient = 2 * correlate(lam, a, 'valid')
   grad = 2.0 * np.correlate(lam.astype(np.float64), a.astype(np.float64), mode='valid')
   # ensure length n
   # Multiplicative update a_new = a * exp(-eta * grad)
   for eta in [eta,0.5*eta,0.25*eta,2*eta,4*eta]:
      logshift=-eta*grad
      logshift -= max to avoid underflow? a zeros cause exp(-inf)=0. Need handle zeros.
      # If a has zeros, a*exp(-eta grad) remains zero; cannot move mass into zeros. Mirror on simplex with strict positivity better. Initialize with tiny positive floor. During updates, keep min positive by adding floor? We can ensure a has small positive everywhere: a = (1-eps)*a + eps/n. But may harm. Use `a_safe = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)`.
      new = a_safe * np.exp(-eta*(grad - grad.min())) # exp stable
      s=new.sum(); if s>0: new/=s else fallback
      score=...
      choose best
   return best_new
```
Multiplicative update only moves mass among currently positive positions; if a has zeros, they stay zero unless we add floor. Add small floor to maintain positivity: Before mirror, `a_work = a.copy(); if min==0: a_work[a_work==0] = 1e-10; renormalize` or `a_work = (1 - 1e-8)*a + 1e-8/n`. This can allow expanding support. But may increase score. We can add `min_mass=1e-10`.

def euclid_grad_step(a, b, eta, vel, beta):
   lam softmax(b/tau); grad=2*correlate(lam,a,'valid')
   vel=beta*vel+(1-beta)*grad
   # line search step fac: cand=project_simplex(a - fac*vel)
```

`softmax_weights(b, tau)`: handle max.
```
def softmax_weights(b, tau):
    if tau <=0: tau=...
    z=b/tau
    z=z-z.max()
    e=np.exp(z)
    s=e.sum()
    if s<=0: w=zeros; w[argmax(b)]=1
    else: w=e/s
    return w
```

def conv_gradient(a, lam):
    n=a.size
    if n<=1: return np.ones(1)
    g=np.correlate(lam.astype(np.float64), a.astype(np.float64), mode='valid')
    if g.size != n:
       tmp=np.zeros(n); tmp[:min(n,g.size)] = g[:min(n,g.size)]; g=tmp
    return 2.0 * g
```

```
def conv_gradient(a, lam):
    n = a.size
    g = np.zeros(n)
    # lam length 2n-1
    # For each i, sum_j lam[i+j] a[j]
    # We can vectorize by for j: g[:2n-1-j] += a[j] * lam[0:2n-1-j]? Wait lam[i+j] for i from 0..2n-2-j.
    for j in range(n):
        aj = a[j]
        if aj != 0.0:
            g[:2*n-1-j] += aj * lam[0:2*n-1-j]
    return 2.0 * g
```

```
def build_conv_matrix(a):
    n = a.size
    A = np.zeros((2*n-1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(n):
        A[i:i+n, i] = a
    return A
```

LP loop specifics:
```
def lp_loop(a, time_budget, rng):
   a=normalize(a); best=a; best_sc=score(a)
   no_improve=0
   t0=time.monotonic()
   while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_budget:
      b=full_conv(a); M=float(b.max()); cur=2nM
      if cur < best_sc: update
      # build A

            vel reset
      if cur < best_sc: update using a? Already.
   return best,best_sc
```

Implement `full_conv` robust:
```
def full_conv(a):
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([0.0])
    if n <= 256:
       return np.convolve(a, a, mode='full')
    size = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a.astype(np.float64), size)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size)[:2*n-1]
```
For n=128 direct. For n=512 use FFT. Full conv from FFT may have tiny negative due round; score max positive; gradient λ softmax with small negatives okay (exp lower). For line search, max maybe tiny negative? no.

return 2.0*float(a.size)*M
```

`softmax_weights(b, tau)`:
```
b=np.asarray(b,float)
if b.size==0: return np.array([])
tau_max=float(np.max(b))
tau = max(1e-18, tau)
z=b/tau
z=z-z.max()
e=np.exp(z)
s=e.sum()
if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=0:
    w=np.zeros(b.size); w[int(np.argmax(b))]=1.0; return w
w=e/s
w[np.isnan(w)] = 0
s=w.sum(); if s>0: w/=s
return w
```

`conv_gradient(a, lam)`:
```
def conv_gradient(a, lam):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n==1:
       return np.array([2.0*float(lam[0])*a[0]] if lam.size else [0.0], dtype=float)
    g=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n):
        aj=float(a[j])
        if aj != 0.0:
            # lam[j:j+n] length n
            g += aj * lam[j:j+n]
    return 2.0 * g
```
This is O(n^2) but safe. For n=512, 512 Python loops per gradient; in gradient polish thousands -> 500k loops, okay. Each loop vector add length512. Fine.
Could optimize with numpy if many zeros, but okay.
Check for j loop: If lam length 2n-1, lam[j:j+n] valid length n for j <= n-1. Yes.
For n=2: g[0]+=a0*lam[0:2]=[a0λ0,a0λ1]; g[1]+=a1*lam[1:3]=[a1λ1,a1λ2]; sum [a0λ0+a1λ1, a0λ1+a1λ2]; factor2. Good.

def euler_step(a,b,vel,eta,beta,rng):
    n=a.size
    M=float(np.max(b)); cur=2*n*M
    tau=0.05*M
    lam=softmax_weights(b,tau)

    # normalize gradient? Maybe divide by n? Previous used eta 0.3, grad scale ~? We can line search.
    vel = beta*vel + (1.0-beta)*grad
    vnorm = np.linalg.norm(vel)
    if vnorm <= 1e-15:
       return a, cur, vel, False
    best_cand=None; best_sc=cur
    # scale step relative to inverse gradient norm: a sum1, variable scale 1/n. Could use step = eta / max(1, vnorm*n?)
    base = eta / max(1.0, float(vnorm))

        step=base*fac
        cand=project_simplex(a - step*vel)

           best_sc=sc; best_cand=cand
    improved = best_cand is not None and best_cand_sc < cur - 1e-12
    return best_cand if improved else None, best_sc, vel, improved
```

```
def mirror_step(a,b,eta,rng):
    n=a.size; M=max; cur=...
    tau=0.05*M; lam=...
    grad=conv_gradient(a,lam) # factor2 maybe
    a_safe=np.maximum(a,1e-12)
    best=None; best_sc=cur
    for fac in (eta,0.5eta,0.25eta,2eta):
       log_shift = -fac * (grad - grad.min())
       # clip for stability: max log_shift 0? use exp
       np.maximum(log_shift, -50) # Actually if grad high, negative large.
       cand = a_safe * np.exp(log_shift)
       cand = normalize_nonnegative(cand)
       sc=score_of(cand)
       if sc < best_sc: best
    return best
```

`lp_direction(a, M, timeout?)`:
```
def lp_direction(a, M):
    n=a.size
    if linprog is None: return a.copy(), 0.0
    A = np.zeros((2*n-1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(n): A[i:i+n, i]=a
    c = -np.ones(n)
    b = M * np.ones(2*n-1)
    try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0, None), method='highs')
    except Exception:
       return a.copy(), 0.0
    if res is None or getattr(res,'x',None) is None:
       return a.copy(), 0.0
    g=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    g=np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
    s=float(g.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
       g=a.copy()
       s=1.0
    return g/s, s
```
Need if linprog with `method='highs'` not installed? catch. `bounds=(0,None)` works for all variables in SciPy.

`mix_line_search(a,g,cur_score)`:
```
def mix_line_search(a,g,cur_score):
    g=normalize_nonnegative(g)
    best=None; best_sc=cur_score
    # t values
    for t in (0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.8,0.9):
       cand=(1.0-t)*a + t*g
       cand=normalize_nonnegative(cand)  # very safe
       sc=score_of(cand)
       if sc < best_sc:
          best_sc=sc; best=cand
    if best is not None and best_sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
       return best, best_sc
    return None, best_sc
```
Could candidate with t=1 (g) have high score; exclude or include. Include but likely bad. If g itself better, jump.
Normalize nonnegative may slightly alter convex sum due clipping, but a,g nonneg, sum1, no need; but safe.

def optimize_one_start(a_init, n, time_budget, rng, use_lp=True):

             else:
                eta*=0.7; a=best + small pert?; if eta<... break
       # symmetrize best
    sy=maybe_symmetrize(best); if lower update
    return best, best_sc

Could be useful. Implement:
```
def resample_pattern(pattern, n):
    if pattern.size == n: return pattern
    if pattern.size <=1: return uniform
    x_old=np.linspace(0,n, pattern.size)
    x_new=np.linspace(0,n,n)
    vals=np.interp(x_new, x_old, pattern)
    vals=np.maximum(vals,0)
    # smooth with small window
    return normalize(vals)
```
Could use for any n. But if previous length 128, we can include init for n=256.

`euler_step` signature:
```
def euler_step(a, b, vel, eta, beta):
    n = a.size
    if n <= 1:
       return None, None, vel
    M = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    cur = 2.0 * n * M
    tau = max(1e-18, 0.05 * M)
    lam = softmax_weights(b, tau)
    grad = conv_gradient(a, lam)
    if grad.size != n:
       grad = np.zeros(n)
    vel = beta * vel + (1.0 - beta) * grad
    vnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(vel))
    if vnorm <= 1e-15:
       return None, cur, vel
    base = eta / max(1.0, vnorm)
    best_cand = None
    best_sc = cur
    for factor in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 2.0, 0.05):
        step = base * factor
        cand = project_simplex(a - step * vel)
        sc = score_of(cand)
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc; best_cand = cand
    if best_cand is not None and best_sc < cur - 1e-12:
        return best_cand, best_sc, vel
    return None, best_sc, vel
```
Note if factor list includes 2 maybe step too big. Good.

`mirror_step`:
```
def mirror_step(a,b,eta):
   n=a.size; cur=...
   M=max; tau=...
   lam=...
   grad=conv_gradient(a,lam)
   if grad.size != n: return None,cur
   a_safe=np.maximum(a,1e-12)
   best_cand=None; best_sc=cur
   for factor in (eta,0.5*eta,0.25*eta,2.0*eta):
       shift = -factor * (grad - grad.min())
       shift = np.clip(shift, -50, 0.0)  # Since exponent positive for below min? If grad-min >=0, -factor* >=0 negative, max 0.
       cand = a_safe * np.exp(shift)
       cand = normalize_nonnegative(cand)
       sc = score_of(cand)
       if sc < best_sc: ...
   return best, best_sc
```
Since shift clipped to <=0, no overflow. `grad - grad.min()` nonnegative. Good. Eta start maybe 2.0.

`lp_direction`: If `linprog is None` return None. Also if n>600 maybe return None to avoid huge. In optimize, use_lp if n<=512.
```
def lp_direction(a, T):
    n=a.size
    if linprog is None or n > 600: return None
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(n): A[i:i+n,i]=a
    c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.full(2*n-1, float(T), dtype=np.float64)
    try:
       res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0, None), method='highs')
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res is None: return None
    x = getattr(res,'x', None)
    if x is None: return None
    if getattr(res,'status',0) == 2: # infeasible
       return None
    g=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
    g=np.where(np.isfinite(g),g,0.0)
    g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
    s=float(g.sum())
    if s <=1e-12:
       return None
    return g/s
```
Potential: status 0 optimal, 3 optimal unbounded? no, 4 infeasible? SciPy status: 0 optimal,1 iteration,2 infeasible,3 unbounded,4 insufficient. If 2 return None; if 3? shouldn't. Check x if status in (0,3). We'll accept any x finite.

`mix_line_search` returns candidate if improves current. It uses `normalize_nonnegative` each candidate (clip, sum). Since a,g sum1 nonneg, no change. But if t>1 not. We'll use t <=1. List t: maybe use sorted? no. For better line search, we can binary? no.
```
def mix_line_search(a,g,cur_score):
   g=normalize_nonnegative(g)
   best=None; best_sc=cur_score
   for t in (0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0,0.05,0.02,0.01):
      cand=(1.0-t)*a+t*g
      cand=normalize_nonnegative(cand)
      sc=score_of(cand)
      if sc < best_sc:
         best_sc=sc; best=cand
   if best is not None and best_sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
      return best,best_sc
   return None,best_sc
```
If t=1 candidate g might improve; okay.

`maybe_symmetrize`:
```
def maybe_symmetrize(a):
   a=normalize_nonnegative(a)
   sym=(a + a[::-1].copy())*0.5
   sym=normalize_nonnegative(sym)
   sc0=score_of(a); sc1=score_of(sym)
   if sc1 +1e-12 < sc0:
       return sym, sc1
   return a, sc0
```
Could call on best at end. It computes score twice. okay.

`init_clusters`: Ensure centers within [0.05,0.95], sigma relative. Add small noise. Returns normalize.
```
def init_clusters(n, rng, centers, sigma_frac):
    i=np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    a=np.zeros(n)
    for c in centers:
        mu=c*(n-1)
        sig=max(1.0, sigma_frac*(n-1))
        a += np.exp(-((i-mu)/sig)**2)
    a += 1e-5 + 0.01 * np.abs(rng.standard_normal(n))
    return normalize_nonnegative(a)
```
If n small, sig. Good.

`init_u`:
```
i=np.arange(n); d=np.minimum(i, n-1-i).astype(float)+1; a=1/np.sqrt(d); a += 1e-6; normalize
```
This high edges. Maybe previous U. Good.

freq = int(2 + rng.integers(0,3))
x=np.arange(n, dtype=float)
a=(1.0 + freq*np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*x/(max(n-1,1))))**2
a += 1e-6 + 0.01*abs(rng.normal)
```
Could produce zeros? square nonnegative.

`get_inits`:
```
def get_inits(n, rng, base_arr):
    inits=[]
    if base_arr is not None and base_arr.size>0:
       pat=np.asarray(base_arr,float)
       if pat.size == n: inits.append(normalize_nonnegative(pat))
       else: inits.append(resample_pattern(pat,n))
    inits.append(init_uniform(n))
    inits.append(init_u(n))
    inits.append(init_clusters(n,rng,[0.22,0.78],0.10))
    inits.append(init_clusters(n,rng,[0.10,0.40,0.60,0.90],0.08))
    inits.append(init_cosine(n,rng))
    inits.append(init_random(n,rng))
    # avoid too many: return first 6? It's 6.
    return inits
```

`optimize_one_start` code refined. Need top-level no closures. It may use `time.monotonic`.

Pseudo:
```
def optimize_one_start(a_init, n, time_budget, rng, use_lp=True):
    if n <=0: return np.array([]), math.inf
    a = normalize_nonnegative(a_init)
    if a.size != n:
       tmp=np.zeros(n); m=min(n,a.size); tmp[:m]=a[:m]; a=normalize_nonnegative(tmp)
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = score_of(a)
    vel = np.zeros(n)
    eta = 0.35
    beta = 0.5
    eta_mirror = 2.0
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total = max(0.0, float(time_budget))
    lp_limit = t0 + (0.65*total if use_lp else 0.0)
    no_lp_improve = 0

    # LP phase
    while use_lp and time.monotonic() < min(lp_limit, t0+total):
        a = normalize_nonnegative(a)
        b = full_conv(a)
        M = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
        cur = 2.0*n*M
        if cur < best_sc:
            best_sc = cur; best = a.copy()
        if M <= 0:
            break
        g = lp_direction(a, M)
        improved = False
        if g is not None:
            cand, sc = mix_line_search(a, g, cur)
            if cand is not None and sc < cur:
                a = cand.copy()
                if sc < best_sc:
                    best_sc = sc; best = a.copy()
                improved = True
        if not improved:
            no_lp_improve += 1
            cand, sc, vel = euler_step(a, b, vel, eta, beta)
            if cand is not None and sc < best_sc:
                a = cand
                vel = vel
                best_sc = sc; best = a.copy()
                no_lp_improve = 0
            elif no_lp_improve >= 5:
                # mirror or perturbation
                cand_m, sc_m = mirror_step(a, b, eta_mirror)
                if cand_m is not None and sc_m < cur - 1e-12:
                    a = cand_m
                    if sc_m < best_sc: update
                    no_lp_improve = 0
                else:
                    a = normalize_nonnegative(best + 0.02 * rng.standard_normal(n))
                    vel = np.zeros(n)
                    eta = 0.2 + 0.3 * rng.random()
                    no_lp_improve = 0
                # occasionally reheat beta
                beta = 0.3 + 0.4 * rng.random()
        else:
            # if LP accepted, maybe adjust eta? not needed
            no_lp_improve = 0

    # Gradient phase until total time
    # start from best to polish, but keep vel zero
    a = best.copy()
    vel = np.zeros(n)
    eta = 0.25
    eta_mirror = 1.5
    stall = 0
    while time.monotonic() < t0 + total:
        a = normalize_nonnegative(a)
        b = full_conv(a)
        cur = 2.0*n*float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
        if cur < best_sc:
            best_sc = cur; best = a.copy(); stall = 0
        cand, sc, vel = euler_step(a, b, vel, eta, beta)
        if cand is not None and sc < cur - 1e-12:
            a = cand
            if sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc; best = a.copy()
            stall = 0
            eta = min(1.0, eta * 1.02)
        else:
            stall += 1
            cand_m, sc_m = mirror_step(a, b, eta_mirror)
            if cand_m is not None and sc_m < cur - 1e-12:
                a = cand_m
                if sc_m < best_sc: update
                stall = 0
            else:
                if stall > 8:
                    a = normalize_nonnegative(best + 0.01 * rng.standard_normal(n))
                    vel = np.zeros(n)
                    eta = 0.1 + 0.3 * rng.random()
                    eta_mirror = 0.5 + 1.5 * rng.random()
                    beta = 0.3 + 0.4 * rng.random()
                    stall = 0
                else:
                    eta *= 0.85
                    eta_mirror *= 0.9
                    if eta < 1e-4 and eta_mirror < 1e-2:
                        break
    # final sym
    a_final, sc_final = maybe_symmetrize(best)
    if sc_final < best_sc:
       best = a_final; best_sc = sc_final
    # also try sym on current if different? maybe
    return best, best_sc
```
Need if `use_lp` and `total` small, lp_limit < t0? okay.

def resample_pattern(pattern, n):
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(pattern), pattern, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p,0.0)
    if p.size == 0 or n == 0: return np.zeros(n)
    if p.size == 1: return np.ones(n)
    if p.size == n: return p
    s=p.sum()
    if s <=0: return np.ones(n)
    p=p/s
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,p.size)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    vals=np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
    # slight smoothing
    ksize=min(5,n)
    if ksize >1 and n>1:
       k=np.ones(ksize)/float(ksize)
       vals=np.convolve(vals,k,mode='same')
    return normalize_nonnegative(vals)
```
